It’s gotten really off—some nights I’m up half the night because my mind won’t slow down, and other times I wake up way too early and feel like I didn’t really rest at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I’ve been unsettled because I’m not sleeping well, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. Lately I’ve been waking up with this really heavy, empty feeling. Even just being in my condo — something I worked hard for — sometimes it just makes me want to cry.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I used to go to salsa class, and I haven’t been going at all. And I like painting, but my paints are still sitting out on the table… I just look at them and feel nothing. It’s like they belong to somebody else.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, salsa used to be fun, and I’ve stopped going. Painting too — the paints are still sitting out, but I just look at them and don’t feel anything. It feels like that stuff belongs to someone else.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, empty feeling?\n\nPatient: About five months, I think. It’s been this depressed, guilty fog for a while, but it got a lot worse after I bought the condo.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still give you some pleasure?\n\nPatient: Honestly, not much. Even the little things don’t really land anymore. I stopped going to salsa, and the paints are still sitting there on the table, but when I look at them I just feel nothing… like they’re not even mine.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any thoughts of death or suicidal ideation?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I don’t think I’ve had clear thoughts like that… not that I’ve noticed. It’s more just feeling empty and tired, but I haven’t been thinking through anything specific like suicide.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks where you felt this low?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s been more than two weeks—more like about five months. That low, guilty fog has kind of been there the whole time, even though it got a lot worse after I bought the condo.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you previously did not enjoy but now find yourself enjoying?\n\nPatient: Not really. I can’t think of anything I used to dislike that I’m suddenly enjoying now. If anything, it’s more like I enjoy less than I used to.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yes. I stopped going to salsa class, and I haven’t touched my paints. They’re still sitting out on the table, but I just look at them and feel nothing—like they belong to somebody else.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. My appetite’s been off—I keep skipping dinner or just eating crackers while I’m looking over my budget notebook. And my work pants have been feeling looser than they did a few weeks ago.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual or feeling like you're speeding up in your thoughts or actions?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I’ve noticed both, kind of. I mean, I’ve been unsettled because I’m not sleeping well, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. Lately I’ve been waking up with this really heavy, empty feeling. Even just being in my condo — something I worked hard for — sometimes it just makes me want to cry.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I used to go to salsa class, and I haven’t been going at all. And I like painting, but my paints are still sitting out on the table… I just look at them and feel nothing. It’s like they belong to somebody else.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, salsa used to be fun, and I’ve stopped going. Painting too — the paints are still sitting out, but I just look at them and don’t feel anything. It feels like that stuff belongs to someone else.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, empty feeling?\n\nPatient: About five months, I think. It’s been this depressed, guilty fog for a while, but it got a lot worse after I bought the condo.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still give you some pleasure?\n\nPatient: Honestly, not much. Even the little things don’t really land anymore. I stopped going to salsa, and the paints are still sitting there on the table, but when I look at them I just feel nothing… like they’re not even mine.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any thoughts of death or suicidal ideation?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I don’t think I’ve had clear thoughts like that… not that I’ve noticed. It’s more just feeling empty and tired, but I haven’t been thinking through anything specific like suicide.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks where you felt this low?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s been more than two weeks—more like about five months. That low, guilty fog has kind of been there the whole time, even though it got a lot worse after I bought the condo.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you previously did not enjoy but now find yourself enjoying?\n\nPatient: Not really. I can’t think of anything I used to dislike that I’m suddenly enjoying now. If anything, it’s more like I enjoy less than I used to.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yes. I stopped going to salsa class, and I haven’t touched my paints. They’re still sitting out on the table, but I just look at them and feel nothing—like they belong to somebody else.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. My appetite’s been off—I keep skipping dinner or just eating crackers while I’m looking over my budget notebook. And my work pants have been feeling looser than they did a few weeks ago.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual or feeling like you're speeding up in your thoughts or actions?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I’ve noticed both, kind of. At work I’ll catch myself moving really slow, like just staring at the calculator before I even press anything. But then other times I’m fidgeting with receipts and I can’t sit still.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from activities most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, most of the time. I mean, salsa used to be fun for me, and now I just don’t go. And my paints are still sitting out at home, but when I look at them, I don’t really feel anything—like they’re not even mine.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or having trouble sitting still most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. I’ll be at work and realize I’m just moving really slow, like staring at the calculator before I press anything.
